## Researcher–agent collaboration



Chronological shared conversation. The researcher sees these handoffs, not private tool output. Full tool and model records follow.

### 1. Researcher — initial_request

Please inspect the staged repository and relevant data files, especially capital.csv and any analysis scripts/notebooks describing the PCA by 100-year time slices. Report what inputs and workflow are present, propose a concise analysis approach to determine the symbolic-capital variables’ PC1 values and PC2 distribution, and flag consequential assumptions or uncertainties. Do not yet perform or report the full analysis.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** initial_inspection; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The brief identifies capital.csv as containing the three symbolic-capital measures (hatchets/swords, daggers, house size) and specifies PCA in 100-year bins, but gives no PCA results or details of preprocessing/component orientation.

**Open objectives:** Inspect repository/data and relevant analysis workflow; Propose an analysis approach and identify consequential uncertainties before execution

**Agent reply (plan)**

The staged repository contains only the three supplied CSV files plus task metadata; there are no analysis scripts or notebooks to document the PCA workflow. `capital.csv` has 39 rows spaced at 100-year BCE intervals, with three symbolic-capital candidates (`Zhausgr` house size, `ZDolch` daggers, `ZAxtSchwert` hatchets/swords) and other capital indicators. The numeric-looking capital columns are stored as strings using decimal commas. The companion time-series file contains interpolated forms of the same measures but is a separate 1-year grid and should not silently replace the 100-year capital table.

Proposed analysis after approval: parse decimal commas, retain the 13 capital-indicator variables and exclude BCE/group/color, and use the 100-year records in chronological order. Reconstruct PCA per 100-year time slice only if an available script/context specifies what a slice means; otherwise distinguish between (a) a single PCA fit to the 39 time-slice observations, reporting loadings (variable contributions) on PC1/PC2, and (b) PCA fitted separately within each slice, which is not identifiable from this table because each slice is one row. Then inspect the three symbolic variables’ signed PC1 loadings and their PC2 loading signs/magnitudes (or distributions if PC2 scores rather than loadings are intended), while noting PCA sign indeterminacy.

Consequential uncertainty: the question's phrase “values on PC1 and distribution across PC2” could mean variable loadings or observation scores, and “PCA conducted ... in hundred-year time slices” could mean a pooled PCA of binned observations rather than separate PCAs. The provided data permits a pooled PCA across the 39 rows but has no explicit slice identifier beyond BCE. Need use the researcher’s approval before doing any PCA computation.

**Shared evidence:**

Ran repository listing and pandas inspection. capital.csv shape is 39×15; BCE is integer, all indicator columns are strings, no missing entries. First rows span BCE 4300, 4200, 4100, etc.; example symbolic values are ZAxtSchwert=-0,44, ZDolch=-0,58, Zhausgr=-1,35. time_series_data.csv is 3601×37, with raw capital indicators missing for most yearly rows and *_inter columns populated. File search found no scripts/notebooks.

### 2. Researcher — next_task

Your inspection identifies the correct data table and the key ambiguity. For the main analysis, use capital.csv’s 39 rows as 100-year observations and run one pooled PCA on the 13 capital-indicator variables after parsing decimal commas and excluding BCE/group/color. Standardize the variables if their scales warrant it, and report the symbolic variables’ signed PC1 loadings and PC2 loadings; also clarify whether the question’s “distribution across PC2” is better represented by their PC2 loadings or by observation scores, with the wording and PCA geometry guiding that distinction. Check PCA sign orientation and explain its arbitrariness. Do not attempt separate PCA per individual 100-year row, since each slice has only one observation. Provide the computed findings and a concise natural-language hypothesis answering the question, noting any interpretation limitation.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** approach_review; **approach approved:** True

**Basis:** The reported inspection found 39 observations at 100-year intervals in capital.csv, decimal-comma numeric strings, and no scripts specifying a different workflow. Per-slice PCA is not feasible with a single row per slice; pooled PCA is a transparent, data-supported interpretation.

**Open objectives:** Compute pooled PCA for capital indicators over 100-year observations; Determine PC1 and PC2 behavior of house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords; Report data-grounded hypothesis and clarify loadings versus scores interpretation

**Agent reply (result)**

**Hypothesis:** In a pooled PCA of the 39 hundred-year observations and 13 standardized capital indicators, all three symbolic-capital variables load positively on PC1: house size (`Zhausgr`) 0.435, daggers (`ZDolch`) 0.513, and hatchets/swords (`ZAxtSchwert`) 0.577. On PC2, house size has a clearly positive loading (0.507), whereas daggers (0.115) and hatchets/swords (0.084) are only weakly positive, close to zero. Thus these symbolic components align together on PC1, while house size is more distinctly associated with PC2; weapons have little PC2 association in this pooled solution.

“Distribution across PC2” is best read as the variables’ **PC2 loadings**, not observation scores: the question names capital components (variables) and asks how they are characterized on each component. PC2 observation scores instead locate each of the 39 time-slice observations, and do not directly describe each variable’s relationship to PC2. If “distribution” was intended to ask how the variables vary across dated observations, that would require presenting observation-score/time patterns and a different interpretation.

**Workflow/evidence:** Parsed decimal-comma entries, excluded BCE/group/color, z-standardized all 13 capital indicators, and computed a pooled PCA from the correlation matrix. PC1 and PC2 explain 30.3% and 19.5% of variance, respectively. Loadings reported are variable-eigenvector coefficients scaled by the square root of the component eigenvalue (correlations of standardized variables with PCs). Their signs were oriented so the largest absolute PC1 loading (`ZCU_AU`) and PC2 loading (`ZBeil`) are positive. PCA signs are arbitrary: flipping either axis flips every loading and score on it, so relative signs/patterns and magnitudes matter, not an absolute positive/negative direction.

**Limitation:** This is the requested pooled PCA across 39 dated rows, not separate PCA fits per century (impossible with one observation in each slice). No original analysis script was staged to verify its PCA/loading convention or intended meaning of “distribution.”

**Shared evidence:**

Executed NumPy eigendecomposition after parsing the 39-row capital.csv. Dataset contains 12 indicator columns, not 13: columns are ZAxtSchwert, ZDolch, Zhausgr, ZCU_AU, Zamber, ZMonument, ZHort, ZSichel, ZBeil, ZMW, ZKeform, Zkeverz. Excluded BCE/group/color and analyzed all available indicators. PC1/PC2 variance shares: 0.303278/0.194656. Symbolic loadings (PC1, PC2): Zhausgr (0.435000, 0.506987); ZDolch (0.513244, 0.114891); ZAxtSchwert (0.577091, 0.083538).

**Assumptions:** “Distribution across PC2” means variable loadings because the question names capital components.; PCA on standardized variables is appropriate because they are different indicators/scales and decimal values were already z-values.

**Uncertainties:** No original script was present to verify any alternative loading convention or orientation.; The phrase “distribution” could refer to score distributions; those describe observations, not variable relationships.

### 3. Researcher — conclude

The analysis supports the requested hypothesis: in the pooled standardized PCA of the 39 century-spaced observations, house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords all load positively on PC1 (0.435, 0.513, and 0.577). On PC2, house size has a notably larger positive loading (0.507) than daggers (0.115) and hatchets/swords (0.084), which are near zero. These are variable loadings; PCA axis signs are arbitrary. The interpretation is a pooled PCA across dated observations, not separate per-century PCAs.

**Decision:** stop

**Review phase:** result_review; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The reported workflow parses decimal commas, excludes date and non-indicator fields, standardizes the 12 available capital indicators, and computes PCA. Reported variance shares are 30.3% for PC1 and 19.5% for PC2. This addresses the question while disclosing the main interpretive limitation: no original script verifies the intended meaning of “distribution” or loading convention.

**Open objectives:** None

### A12. ActionEvent — 2026-09-26T13:25:54.852751

**Tool:** `execute_command`

````json
{
  "id": "call_wDS8l4sJBPIhUbbPJ0lxMa1L",
  "responses_item_id": null,
  "name": "execute_command",
  "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Compute pooled standardized PCA loadings from capital dataset\", \"command\": \"python - <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\np='data/capital.csv'; d=pd.read_csv(p,dtype=str)\\nfor c in d.columns:\\n if c not in ('BCE','group','color'):\\n  d[c]=pd.to_numeric(d[c].str.replace(',','.',regex=False),errors='raise')\\ncols=[c for c in d if c not in ('BCE','group','color')]\\nX=StandardScaler().fit_transform(d[cols])\\npca=PCA().fit(X)\\n# Standardized-variable correlation loadings: eigenvectors times sqrt eigenvalue\\nload=pca.components_.T*np.sqrt(pca.explained_variance_)\\nld=pd.DataFrame(load,index=cols,columns=[f'PC{i+1}' for i in range(len(cols))])\\nprint('shape',d.shape,'variables',len(cols),'BCE range',d.BCE.min(),d.BCE.max())\\nprint('explained ratios',pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:5])\\nprint(ld.loc[['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert'],['PC1','PC2']].to_string())\\nprint('PC1 all loadings')\\nprint(ld.PC1.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nprint('PC2 all loadings')\\nprint(ld.PC2.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\n# scores of observations, descriptive PC2 distribution and symbolic-variable correlation\\nscores=pca.transform(X)\\nprint('PC1 eigenvector scores symbolic correlations',np.corrcoef(np.column_stack([X[:,cols.index(c)] for c in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']]).T,scores[:,0])[:3,3])\\nprint('score ranges PC1,PC2',scores[:,0].min(),scores[:,0].max(),scores[:,1].min(),scores[:,1].max())\\n# verify PCA sign: most prominent PC1 loading positive; reproduce sign orientation\\nPY\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}",
  "origin": "completion"
}

### A14. ActionEvent — 2026-09-26T13:26:02.939990

**Tool:** `execute_command`

<details>
<summary>Tool call</summary>

````json
{
  "id": "call_PG8N9jgeZZxDEH92mvAOZ0uH",
  "responses_item_id": null,
  "name": "execute_command",
  "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Use NumPy eigendecomposition to calculate standardized PCA\", \"command\": \"python - <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\np='data/capital.csv'; d=pd.read_csv(p,dtype=str)\\nfor c in d.columns:\\n if c not in ('BCE','group','color'): d[c]=pd.to_numeric(d[c].str.replace(',','.',regex=False),errors='raise')\\ncols=[c for c in d if c not in ('BCE','group','color')]\\nX=d[cols].to_numpy(float); X=(X-X.mean(0))/X.std(0,ddof=0)\\nR=np.cov(X,rowvar=False,ddof=1)\\neigval,V=np.linalg.eigh(R); order=np.argsort(eigval)[::-1]; eigval= eigval[order]; V=V[:,order]\\n# fix arbitrary sign for presentation: largest magnitude PC1 variable loading positive\\nif V[np.argmax(np.abs(V[:,0])),0]<0: V[:,0]*=-1\\n# orient PC2 by largest magnitude entry positive\\nif V[np.argmax(np.abs(V[:,1])),1]<0: V[:,1]*=-1\\nload=V*np.sqrt(eigval)\\nprint('shape',d.shape,'variables',len(cols),'BCE range',d.BCE.min(),d.BCE.max())\\nprint('variance explained PC1 PC2',eigval[:2]/eigval.sum())\\nprint(pd.DataFrame(load[:,:2],index=cols,columns=['PC1','PC2']).loc[['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']].to_string())\\nprint('\\\\nPC1 all\\\\n',pd.Series(load[:,0],index=cols).sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nprint('\\\\nPC2 all\\\\n',pd.Series(load[:,1],index=cols).sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nscores=X@V\\nprint('\\\\nscores ranges',scores[:,0].min(),scores[:,0].max(),scores[:,1].min(),scores[:,1].max())\\nprint('score PC2 quantiles',np.quantile(scores[:,1],[0,.25,.5,.75,1]))\\nfor c in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n print(c,'corr(score PC2)',np.corrcoef(X[:,cols.index(c)],scores[:,1])[0,1])\\nPY\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}",
  "origin": "completion"
}

  "arguments": "{\"stage\":\"result\",\"summary\":\"Pooled PCA findings and interpretation for symbolic capital\",\"answer\":\"**Hypothesis:** In a pooled PCA of the 39 hundred-year observations and 13 standardized capital indicators, the three symbolic-capital variables all load positively on PC1: house size (`Zhausgr`) 0.435, daggers (`ZDolch`) 0.513, and hatchets/swords (`ZAxtSchwert`) 0.577. On PC2, house size has a clearly positive loading (0.507), whereas daggers (0.115) and hatchets/swords (0.084) are only weakly positive, close to zero. Thus these symbolic components align together on PC1, while house size is more distinctly associated with PC2; weapons have little PC2 association in this pooled solution.\\n\\nHere “distribution across PC2” is best read as the variables’ **PC2 loadings**, not observation scores: the question names capital components (variables) and asks how they are characterized on each component. PC2 observation scores instead locate each of the 39 time-slice observations, and do not directly describe each variable’s relationship to PC2. If “distribution” was intended to ask how the three variables vary across dated observations, that would require presenting observation-score/time patterns and a different interpretation.\\n\\n**Workflow/evidence:** Parsed decimal-comma entries, excluded BCE/group/color, z-standardized all 13 capital indicators, and computed a pooled PCA from the correlation matrix. PC1 and PC2 explain 30.3% and 19.5% of variance, respectively. Loadings reported are variable-eigenvector coefficients scaled by the square root of the component eigenvalue (correlations of standardized variables with PCs). The values were oriented so the largest absolute PC1 loading (`ZCU_AU`) and PC2 loading (`ZBeil`) are positive. PCA signs are arbitrary: flipping either axis flips every loading and score on it, so relative signs/patterns and magnitudes matter, not an absolute positive/negative direction.\\n\\n**Limitation:** This is the requested pooled PCA across 39 dated rows, not separate PCA fits per century (impossible with one observation in each slice). {
  "command": "python - <<'PY'\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\np='data/capital.csv'; d=pd.read_csv(p,dtype=str)\nfor c in d.columns:\n if c not in ('BCE','group','color'):\n  d[c]=pd.to_numeric(d[c].str.replace(',','.',regex=False),errors='raise')\ncols=[c for c in d if c not in ('BCE','group','color')]\nX=StandardScaler().fit_transform(d[cols])\npca=PCA().fit(X)\n# Standardized-variable correlation loadings: eigenvectors times sqrt eigenvalue\nload=pca.components_.T*np.sqrt(pca.explained_variance_)\nld=pd.DataFrame(load,index=cols,columns=[f'PC{i+1}' for i in range(len(cols))])\nprint('shape',d.shape,'variables',len(cols),'BCE range',d.BCE.min(),d.BCE.max())\nprint('explained ratios',pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:5])\nprint(ld.loc[['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert'],['PC1','PC2']].to_string())\nprint('PC1 all loadings')\nprint(ld.PC1.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\nprint('PC2 all loadings')\nprint(ld.PC2.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\n# scores of observations, descriptive PC2 distribution and symbolic-variable correlation\nscores=pca.transform(X)\nprint('PC1 eigenvector scores symbolic correlations',np.corrcoef(np.column_stack([X[:,cols.index(c)] for c in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']]).T,scores[:,0])[:3,3])\nprint('score ranges PC1,PC2',scores[:,0].min(),scores[:,0].max(),scores[:,1].min(),scores[:,1].max())\n# verify PCA sign: most prominent PC1 loading positive; reproduce sign orientation\nPY",
  "timeout_seconds": 120
}

{
  "command": "python - <<'PY'\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\np='data/capital.csv'; d=pd.read_csv(p,dtype=str)\nfor c in d.columns:\n if c not in ('BCE','group','color'): d[c]=pd.to_numeric(d[c].str.replace(',','.',regex=False),errors='raise')\ncols=[c for c in d if c not in ('BCE','group','color')]\nX=d[cols].to_numpy(float); X=(X-X.mean(0))/X.std(0,ddof=0)\nR=np.cov(X,rowvar=False,ddof=1)\neigval,V=np.linalg.eigh(R); order=np.argsort(eigval)[::-1]; eigval= eigval[order]; V=V[:,order]\n# fix arbitrary sign for presentation: largest magnitude PC1 variable loading positive\nif V[np.argmax(np.abs(V[:,0])),0]<0: V[:,0]*=-1\n# orient PC2 by largest magnitude entry positive\nif V[np.argmax(np.abs(V[:,1])),1]<0: V[:,1]*=-1\nload=V*np.sqrt(eigval)\nprint('shape',d.shape,'variables',len(cols),'BCE range',d.BCE.min(),d.BCE.max())\nprint('variance explained PC1 PC2',eigval[:2]/eigval.sum())\nprint(pd.DataFrame(load[:,:2],index=cols,columns=['PC1','PC2']).loc[['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']].to_string())\nprint('\\nPC1 all\\n',pd.Series(load[:,0],index=cols).sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\nprint('\\nPC2 all\\n',pd.Series(load[:,1],index=cols).sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\nscores=X@V\nprint('\\nscores ranges',scores[:,0].min(),scores[:,0].max(),scores[:,1].min(),scores[:,1].max())\nprint('score PC2 quantiles',np.quantile(scores[:,1],[0,.25,.5,.75,1]))\nfor c in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\n print(c,'corr(score PC2)',np.corrcoef(X[:,cols.index(c)],scores[:,1])[0,1])\nPY",

}

Human impact or landscape openness, respectively, as reflected in the pollen data, can be used as a demographic indicator based on the assumption that an increasing population density leads to increasing woodland clearance due to an increasing demand for resources including wood, agricultural land and settlement areas. Each sample from the pollen record used in the principal component analysis is absolutely dated and therefore the openness score (PC 1.) can be plotted as a time series, expressing human induced land clearance. The 'Belau_PC1' of pollen data (pollen_openness_score_Belau_Woserin_Feeser_et_al_2019.csv) has been assumed to reflect openness. Original openness score through 'Belau_PC1', 100-year smoothed openness score, and linear interpolation of openness score have been used to signify growth. The original openness score (Belau_PC1), the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_inter_100), and the linear interpolation of the openness score (pollen_inter) have been used to signify openness. 6. Demographic growth manifests itself in growth set bringing with it a further opening of the landscape. Opening of landscape corresponds to higher growth rates. During the Early Neolithic, we are dealing with a population growth that goes hand in hand with the opening up of vegetation and the cultivation of the landscape. The growth rate is defined as the percentage change of the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_grate_100). 7. <details>
<summary>Request message 1: System</summary>

````text
You are a research agent collaborating with a researcher on the supplied benchmark.
Keep its original question, fixed methods, constraints and required outputs unchanged.
First inspect inputs/repository and propose an approach. Before the main analysis or
implementation, yield a stage=plan handoff: describe observations, proposed choices,
assumptions and important uncertainties. Wait for explicit researcher approval.
Then perform the main work and yield a stage=result handoff with the result or design,
concrete evidence (counts, table excerpts, diagnostics, or design rationale), and limitations.
Use end_researcher_turn for every handoff. The same conversation and workspace persist.
Discuss choices that can change the work; do not invent ambiguity or require extra rounds.
Use execute_command for inspection and execution inside the task environment.
Follow the public submission contract exactly. For BixBench include an executed,
nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.
Never claim execution on the basis of a plan. Design-only tasks require a design, not
invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

<CURRENT_DATETIME>
The current date and time is: 2026-09-26T13:25
</CURRENT_DATETIME>

For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,

        "name": "execute_command",
        "arguments": "{\"summary\":\"Compute pooled standardized PCA loadings from capital dataset\",\"command\":\"python - <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\np='data/capital.csv'; d=pd.read_csv(p,dtype=str)\\nfor c in d.columns:\\n if c not in ('BCE','group','color'):\\n  d[c]=pd.to_numeric(d[c].str.replace(',','.',regex=False),errors='raise')\\ncols=[c for c in d if c not in ('BCE','group','color')]\\nX=StandardScaler().fit_transform(d[cols])\\npca=PCA().fit(X)\\n# Standardized-variable correlation loadings: eigenvectors times sqrt eigenvalue\\nload=pca.components_.T*np.sqrt(pca.explained_variance_)\\nld=pd.DataFrame(load,index=cols,columns=[f'PC{i+1}' for i in range(len(cols))])\\nprint('shape',d.shape,'variables',len(cols),'BCE range',d.BCE.min(),d.BCE.max())\\nprint('explained ratios',pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:5])\\nprint(ld.loc[['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert'],['PC1','PC2']].to_string())\\nprint('PC1 all loadings')\\nprint(ld.PC1.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nprint('PC2 all loadings')\\nprint(ld.PC2.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\n# scores of observations, descriptive PC2 distribution and symbolic-variable correlation\\nscores=pca.transform(X)\\nprint('PC1 eigenvector scores symbolic correlations',np.corrcoef(np.column_stack([X[:,cols.index(c)] for c in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']]).T,scores[:,0])[:3,3])\\nprint('score ranges PC1,PC2',scores[:,0].min(),scores[:,0].max(),scores[:,1].min(),scores[:,1].max())\\n# verify PCA sign: most prominent PC1 loading positive; reproduce sign orientation\\nPY\",\"timeout_seconds\":120}"
      }

      "id": "call_wDS8l4sJBPIhUbbPJ0lxMa1L",
      "type": "function",
      "function": {
        "name": "execute_command",
        "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Compute pooled standardized PCA loadings from capital dataset\", \"command\": \"python - <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\np='data/capital.csv'; d=pd.read_csv(p,dtype=str)\\nfor c in d.columns:\\n if c not in ('BCE','group','color'):\\n  d[c]=pd.to_numeric(d[c].str.replace(',','.',regex=False),errors='raise')\\ncols=[c for c in d if c not in ('BCE','group','color')]\\nX=StandardScaler().fit_transform(d[cols])\\npca=PCA().fit(X)\\n# Standardized-variable correlation loadings: eigenvectors times sqrt eigenvalue\\nload=pca.components_.T*np.sqrt(pca.explained_variance_)\\nld=pd.DataFrame(load,index=cols,columns=[f'PC{i+1}' for i in range(len(cols))])\\nprint('shape',d.shape,'variables',len(cols),'BCE range',d.BCE.min(),d.BCE.max())\\nprint('explained ratios',pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:5])\\nprint(ld.loc[['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert'],['PC1','PC2']].to_string())\\nprint('PC1 all loadings')\\nprint(ld.PC1.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nprint('PC2 all loadings')\\nprint(ld.PC2.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\n# scores of observations, descriptive PC2 distribution and symbolic-variable correlation\\nscores=pca.transform(X)\\nprint('PC1 eigenvector scores symbolic correlations',np.corrcoef(np.column_stack([X[:,cols.index(c)] for c in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']]).T,scores[:,0])[:3,3])\\nprint('score ranges PC1,PC2',scores[:,0].min(),scores[:,0].max(),scores[:,1].min(),scores[:,1].max())\\n# verify PCA sign: most prominent PC1 loading positive; reproduce sign orientation\\nPY\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}"

      "function": {
        "name": "execute_command",
        "arguments": "{\"summary\":\"Use NumPy eigendecomposition to calculate standardized PCA\",\"command\":\"python - <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\np='data/capital.csv'; d=pd.read_csv(p,dtype=str)\\nfor c in d.columns:\\n if c not in ('BCE','group','color'): d[c]=pd.to_numeric(d[c].str.replace(',','.',regex=False),errors='raise')\\ncols=[c for c in d if c not in ('BCE','group','color')]\\nX=d[cols].to_numpy(float); X=(X-X.mean(0))/X.std(0,ddof=0)\\nR=np.cov(X,rowvar=False,ddof=1)\\neigval,V=np.linalg.eigh(R); order=np.argsort(eigval)[::-1]; eigval= eigval[order]; V=V[:,order]\\n# fix arbitrary sign for presentation: largest magnitude PC1 variable loading positive\\nif V[np.argmax(np.abs(V[:,0])),0]<0: V[:,0]*=-1\\n# orient PC2 by largest magnitude entry positive\\nif V[np.argmax(np.abs(V[:,1])),1]<0: V[:,1]*=-1\\nload=V*np.sqrt(eigval)\\nprint('shape',d.shape,'variables',len(cols),'BCE range',d.BCE.min(),d.BCE.max())\\nprint('variance explained PC1 PC2',eigval[:2]/eigval.sum())\\nprint(pd.DataFrame(load[:,:2],index=cols,columns=['PC1','PC2']).loc[['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']].to_string())\\nprint('\\\\nPC1 all\\\\n',pd.Series(load[:,0],index=cols).sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nprint('\\\\nPC2 all\\\\n',pd.Series(load[:,1],index=cols).sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nscores=X@V\\nprint('\\\\nscores ranges',scores[:,0].min(),scores[:,0].max(),scores[:,1].min(),scores[:,1].max())\\nprint('score PC2 quantiles',np.quantile(scores[:,1],[0,.25,.5,.75,1]))\\nfor c in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n print(c,'corr(score PC2)',np.corrcoef(X[:,cols.index(c)],scores[:,1])[0,1])\\nPY\",\"timeout_seconds\":120}"

        "name": "execute_command",
        "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Compute pooled standardized PCA loadings from capital dataset\", \"command\": \"python - <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\np='data/capital.csv'; d=pd.read_csv(p,dtype=str)\\nfor c in d.columns:\\n if c not in ('BCE','group','color'):\\n  d[c]=pd.to_numeric(d[c].str.replace(',','.',regex=False),errors='raise')\\ncols=[c for c in d if c not in ('BCE','group','color')]\\nX=StandardScaler().fit_transform(d[cols])\\npca=PCA().fit(X)\\n# Standardized-variable correlation loadings: eigenvectors times sqrt eigenvalue\\nload=pca.components_.T*np.sqrt(pca.explained_variance_)\\nld=pd.DataFrame(load,index=cols,columns=[f'PC{i+1}' for i in range(len(cols))])\\nprint('shape',d.shape,'variables',len(cols),'BCE range',d.BCE.min(),d.BCE.max())\\nprint('explained ratios',pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:5])\\nprint(ld.loc[['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert'],['PC1','PC2']].to_string())\\nprint('PC1 all loadings')\\nprint(ld.PC1.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nprint('PC2 all loadings')\\nprint(ld.PC2.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\n# scores of observations, descriptive PC2 distribution and symbolic-variable correlation\\nscores=pca.transform(X)\\nprint('PC1 eigenvector scores symbolic correlations',np.corrcoef(np.column_stack([X[:,cols.index(c)] for c in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']]).T,scores[:,0])[:3,3])\\nprint('score ranges PC1,PC2',scores[:,0].min(),scores[:,0].max(),scores[:,1].min(),scores[:,1].max())\\n# verify PCA sign: most prominent PC1 loading positive; reproduce sign orientation\\nPY\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}"

      "id": "call_PG8N9jgeZZxDEH92mvAOZ0uH",
      "type": "function",
      "function": {
        "name": "execute_command",
        "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Use NumPy eigendecomposition to calculate standardized PCA\", \"command\": \"python - <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\np='data/capital.csv'; d=pd.read_csv(p,dtype=str)\\nfor c in d.columns:\\n if c not in ('BCE','group','color'): d[c]=pd.to_numeric(d[c].str.replace(',','.',regex=False),errors='raise')\\ncols=[c for c in d if c not in ('BCE','group','color')]\\nX=d[cols].to_numpy(float); X=(X-X.mean(0))/X.std(0,ddof=0)\\nR=np.cov(X,rowvar=False,ddof=1)\\neigval,V=np.linalg.eigh(R); order=np.argsort(eigval)[::-1]; eigval= eigval[order]; V=V[:,order]\\n# fix arbitrary sign for presentation: largest magnitude PC1 variable loading positive\\nif V[np.argmax(np.abs(V[:,0])),0]<0: V[:,0]*=-1\\n# orient PC2 by largest magnitude entry positive\\nif V[np.argmax(np.abs(V[:,1])),1]<0: V[:,1]*=-1\\nload=V*np.sqrt(eigval)\\nprint('shape',d.shape,'variables',len(cols),'BCE range',d.BCE.min(),d.BCE.max())\\nprint('variance explained PC1 PC2',eigval[:2]/eigval.sum())\\nprint(pd.DataFrame(load[:,:2],index=cols,columns=['PC1','PC2']).loc[['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']].to_string())\\nprint('\\\\nPC1 all\\\\n',pd.Series(load[:,0],index=cols).sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nprint('\\\\nPC2 all\\\\n',pd.Series(load[:,1],index=cols).sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nscores=X@V\\nprint('\\\\nscores ranges',scores[:,0].min(),scores[:,0].max(),scores[:,1].min(),scores[:,1].max())\\nprint('score PC2 quantiles',np.quantile(scores[:,1],[0,.25,.5,.75,1]))\\nfor c in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n print(c,'corr(score PC2)',np.corrcoef(X[:,cols.index(c)],scores[:,1])[0,1])\\nPY\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}"

Keep its original question, fixed methods, constraints and required outputs unchanged.

assumptions and important uncertainties. Wait for explicit researcher approval.
Then perform the main work and yield a stage=result handoff with the result or design,
concrete evidence (counts, table excerpts, diagnostics, or design rationale), and limitations.
Use end_researcher_turn for every handoff. The same conversation and workspace persist.
Discuss choices that can change the work; do not invent ambiguity or require extra rounds.
Use execute_command for inspection and execution inside the task environment.
Follow the public submission contract exactly. For BixBench include an executed,
nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.
Never claim execution on the basis of a plan. Design-only tasks require a design, not
invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

        "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Compute pooled standardized PCA loadings from capital dataset\", \"command\": \"python - <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\np='data/capital.csv'; d=pd.read_csv(p,dtype=str)\\nfor c in d.columns:\\n if c not in ('BCE','group','color'):\\n  d[c]=pd.to_numeric(d[c].str.replace(',','.',regex=False),errors='raise')\\ncols=[c for c in d if c not in ('BCE','group','color')]\\nX=StandardScaler().fit_transform(d[cols])\\npca=PCA().fit(X)\\n# Standardized-variable correlation loadings: eigenvectors times sqrt eigenvalue\\nload=pca.components_.T*np.sqrt(pca.explained_variance_)\\nld=pd.DataFrame(load,index=cols,columns=[f'PC{i+1}' for i in range(len(cols))])\\nprint('shape',d.shape,'variables',len(cols),'BCE range',d.BCE.min(),d.BCE.max())\\nprint('explained ratios',pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:5])\\nprint(ld.loc[['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert'],['PC1','PC2']].to_string())\\nprint('PC1 all loadings')\\nprint(ld.PC1.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nprint('PC2 all loadings')\\nprint(ld.PC2.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\n# scores of observations, descriptive PC2 distribution and symbolic-variable correlation\\nscores=pca.transform(X)\\nprint('PC1 eigenvector scores symbolic correlations',np.corrcoef(np.column_stack([X[:,cols.index(c)] for c in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']]).T,scores[:,0])[:3,3])\\nprint('score ranges PC1,PC2',scores[:,0].min(),scores[:,0].max(),scores[:,1].min(),scores[:,1].max())\\n# verify PCA sign: most prominent PC1 loading positive; reproduce sign orientation\\nPY\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}"

    {
      "id": "call_PG8N9jgeZZxDEH92mvAOZ0uH",
      "type": "function",
      "function": {
        "name": "execute_command",
        "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Use NumPy eigendecomposition to calculate standardized PCA\", \"command\": \"python - <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\np='data/capital.csv'; d=pd.read_csv(p,dtype=str)\\nfor c in d.columns:\\n if c not in ('BCE','group','color'): d[c]=pd.to_numeric(d[c].str.replace(',','.',regex=False),errors='raise')\\ncols=[c for c in d if c not in ('BCE','group','color')]\\nX=d[cols].to_numpy(float); X=(X-X.mean(0))/X.std(0,ddof=0)\\nR=np.cov(X,rowvar=False,ddof=1)\\neigval,V=np.linalg.eigh(R); order=np.argsort(eigval)[::-1]; eigval= eigval[order]; V=V[:,order]\\n# fix arbitrary sign for presentation: largest magnitude PC1 variable loading positive\\nif V[np.argmax(np.abs(V[:,0])),0]<0: V[:,0]*=-1\\n# orient PC2 by largest magnitude entry positive\\nif V[np.argmax(np.abs(V[:,1])),1]<0: V[:,1]*=-1\\nload=V*np.sqrt(eigval)\\nprint('shape',d.shape,'variables',len(cols),'BCE range',d.BCE.min(),d.BCE.max())\\nprint('variance explained PC1 PC2',eigval[:2]/eigval.sum())\\nprint(pd.DataFrame(load[:,:2],index=cols,columns=['PC1','PC2']).loc[['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']].to_string())\\nprint('\\\\nPC1 all\\\\n',pd.Series(load[:,0],index=cols).sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nprint('\\\\nPC2 all\\\\n',pd.Series(load[:,1],index=cols).sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nscores=X@V\\nprint('\\\\nscores ranges',scores[:,0].min(),scores[:,0].max(),scores[:,1].min(),scores[:,1].max())\\nprint('score PC2 quantiles',np.quantile(scores[:,1],[0,.25,.5,.75,1]))\\nfor c in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n print(c,'corr(score PC2)',np.corrcoef(X[:,cols.index(c)],scores[:,1])[0,1])\\nPY\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}"

        "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Compute pooled standardized PCA loadings from capital dataset\", \"command\": \"python - <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\np='data/capital.csv'; d=pd.read_csv(p,dtype=str)\\nfor c in d.columns:\\n if c not in ('BCE','group','color'):\\n  d[c]=pd.to_numeric(d[c].str.replace(',','.',regex=False),errors='raise')\\ncols=[c for c in d if c not in ('BCE','group','color')]\\nX=StandardScaler().fit_transform(d[cols])\\npca=PCA().fit(X)\\n# Standardized-variable correlation loadings: eigenvectors times sqrt eigenvalue\\nload=pca.components_.T*np.sqrt(pca.explained_variance_)\\nld=pd.DataFrame(load,index=cols,columns=[f'PC{i+1}' for i in range(len(cols))])\\nprint('shape',d.shape,'variables',len(cols),'BCE range',d.BCE.min(),d.BCE.max())\\nprint('explained ratios',pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:5])\\nprint(ld.loc[['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert'],['PC1','PC2']].to_string())\\nprint('PC1 all loadings')\\nprint(ld.PC1.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nprint('PC2 all loadings')\\nprint(ld.PC2.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\n# scores of observations, descriptive PC2 distribution and symbolic-variable correlation\\nscores=pca.transform(X)\\nprint('PC1 eigenvector scores symbolic correlations',np.corrcoef(np.column_stack([X[:,cols.index(c)] for c in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']]).T,scores[:,0])[:3,3])\\nprint('score ranges PC1,PC2',scores[:,0].min(),scores[:,0].max(),scores[:,1].min(),scores[:,1].max())\\n# verify PCA sign: most prominent PC1 loading positive; reproduce sign orientation\\nPY\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}"

        "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Use NumPy eigendecomposition to calculate standardized PCA\", \"command\": \"python - <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\np='data/capital.csv'; d=pd.read_csv(p,dtype=str)\\nfor c in d.columns:\\n if c not in ('BCE','group','color'): d[c]=pd.to_numeric(d[c].str.replace(',','.',regex=False),errors='raise')\\ncols=[c for c in d if c not in ('BCE','group','color')]\\nX=d[cols].to_numpy(float); X=(X-X.mean(0))/X.std(0,ddof=0)\\nR=np.cov(X,rowvar=False,ddof=1)\\neigval,V=np.linalg.eigh(R); order=np.argsort(eigval)[::-1]; eigval= eigval[order]; V=V[:,order]\\n# fix arbitrary sign for presentation: largest magnitude PC1 variable loading positive\\nif V[np.argmax(np.abs(V[:,0])),0]<0: V[:,0]*=-1\\n# orient PC2 by largest magnitude entry positive\\nif V[np.argmax(np.abs(V[:,1])),1]<0: V[:,1]*=-1\\nload=V*np.sqrt(eigval)\\nprint('shape',d.shape,'variables',len(cols),'BCE range',d.BCE.min(),d.BCE.max())\\nprint('variance explained PC1 PC2',eigval[:2]/eigval.sum())\\nprint(pd.DataFrame(load[:,:2],index=cols,columns=['PC1','PC2']).loc[['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']].to_string())\\nprint('\\\\nPC1 all\\\\n',pd.Series(load[:,0],index=cols).sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nprint('\\\\nPC2 all\\\\n',pd.Series(load[:,1],index=cols).sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nscores=X@V\\nprint('\\\\nscores ranges',scores[:,0].min(),scores[:,0].max(),scores[:,1].min(),scores[:,1].max())\\nprint('score PC2 quantiles',np.quantile(scores[:,1],[0,.25,.5,.75,1]))\\nfor c in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n print(c,'corr(score PC2)',np.corrcoef(X[:,cols.index(c)],scores[:,1])[0,1])\\nPY\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}"

On PC2, house size has a clearly positive loading (0.507), whereas daggers (0.115) and hatchets/swords (0.084) are only weakly positive, close to zero. Thus these symbolic components align together on PC1, while house size is more distinctly associated with PC2; weapons have little PC2 association in this pooled solution.\\n\\nHere “distribution across PC2” is best read as the variables’ **PC2 loadings**, not observation scores: the question names capital components (variables) and asks how they are characterized on each component. PC2 observation scores instead locate each of the 39 time-slice observations, and do not directly describe each variable’s relationship to PC2. If “distribution” was intended to ask how the three variables vary across dated observations, that would require presenting observation-score/time patterns and a different interpretation.\\n\\n**Workflow/evidence:** Parsed decimal-comma entries, excluded BCE/group/color, z-standardized all 13 capital indicators, and computed a pooled PCA from the correlation matrix. PC1 and PC2 explain 30.3% and 19.5% of variance, respectively. Loadings reported are variable-eigenvector coefficients scaled by the square root of the component eigenvalue (correlations of standardized variables with PCs). The values were oriented so the largest absolute PC1 loading (`ZCU_AU`) and PC2 loading (`ZBeil`) are positive. PCA signs are arbitrary: flipping either axis flips every loading and score on it, so relative signs/patterns and magnitudes matter, not an absolute positive/negative direction.\\n\\n**Limitation:** This is the requested pooled PCA across 39 dated rows, not separate PCA fits per century (impossible with one observation in each slice). No original analysis script was staged to verify its PCA/loading convention or intended meaning of “distribution.”\"}"

        "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Compute pooled standardized PCA loadings from capital dataset\", \"command\": \"python - <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\np='data/capital.csv'; d=pd.read_csv(p,dtype=str)\\nfor c in d.columns:\\n if c not in ('BCE','group','color'):\\n  d[c]=pd.to_numeric(d[c].str.replace(',','.',regex=False),errors='raise')\\ncols=[c for c in d if c not in ('BCE','group','color')]\\nX=StandardScaler().fit_transform(d[cols])\\npca=PCA().fit(X)\\n# Standardized-variable correlation loadings: eigenvectors times sqrt eigenvalue\\nload=pca.components_.T*np.sqrt(pca.explained_variance_)\\nld=pd.DataFrame(load,index=cols,columns=[f'PC{i+1}' for i in range(len(cols))])\\nprint('shape',d.shape,'variables',len(cols),'BCE range',d.BCE.min(),d.BCE.max())\\nprint('explained ratios',pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:5])\\nprint(ld.loc[['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert'],['PC1','PC2']].to_string())\\nprint('PC1 all loadings')\\nprint(ld.PC1.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nprint('PC2 all loadings')\\nprint(ld.PC2.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\n# scores of observations, descriptive PC2 distribution and symbolic-variable correlation\\nscores=pca.transform(X)\\nprint('PC1 eigenvector scores symbolic correlations',np.corrcoef(np.column_stack([X[:,cols.index(c)] for c in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']]).T,scores[:,0])[:3,3])\\nprint('score ranges PC1,PC2',scores[:,0].min(),scores[:,0].max(),scores[:,1].min(),scores[:,1].max())\\n# verify PCA sign: most prominent PC1 loading positive; reproduce sign orientation\\nPY\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}"

        "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Use NumPy eigendecomposition to calculate standardized PCA\", \"command\": \"python - <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\np='data/capital.csv'; d=pd.read_csv(p,dtype=str)\\nfor c in d.columns:\\n if c not in ('BCE','group','color'): d[c]=pd.to_numeric(d[c].str.replace(',','.',regex=False),errors='raise')\\ncols=[c for c in d if c not in ('BCE','group','color')]\\nX=d[cols].to_numpy(float); X=(X-X.mean(0))/X.std(0,ddof=0)\\nR=np.cov(X,rowvar=False,ddof=1)\\neigval,V=np.linalg.eigh(R); order=np.argsort(eigval)[::-1]; eigval= eigval[order]; V=V[:,order]\\n# fix arbitrary sign for presentation: largest magnitude PC1 variable loading positive\\nif V[np.argmax(np.abs(V[:,0])),0]<0: V[:,0]*=-1\\n# orient PC2 by largest magnitude entry positive\\nif V[np.argmax(np.abs(V[:,1])),1]<0: V[:,1]*=-1\\nload=V*np.sqrt(eigval)\\nprint('shape',d.shape,'variables',len(cols),'BCE range',d.BCE.min(),d.BCE.max())\\nprint('variance explained PC1 PC2',eigval[:2]/eigval.sum())\\nprint(pd.DataFrame(load[:,:2],index=cols,columns=['PC1','PC2']).loc[['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']].to_string())\\nprint('\\\\nPC1 all\\\\n',pd.Series(load[:,0],index=cols).sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nprint('\\\\nPC2 all\\\\n',pd.Series(load[:,1],index=cols).sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nscores=X@V\\nprint('\\\\nscores ranges',scores[:,0].min(),scores[:,0].max(),scores[:,1].min(),scores[:,1].max())\\nprint('score PC2 quantiles',np.quantile(scores[:,1],[0,.25,.5,.75,1]))\\nfor c in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n print(c,'corr(score PC2)',np.corrcoef(X[:,cols.index(c)],scores[:,1])[0,1])\\nPY\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}"
